### Corporate culture



Co-founder [Steve Wozniak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Wozniak) and Macintosh engineer [Andy Hertzfeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Hertzfeld) attended the [Apple User Group Connection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_User_Group_Connection) club in 1985.



Universities that produced the most alumni who became Apple employees

Apple is one of several highly successful companies founded in the 1970s that bucked the traditional notions of [corporate culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corporate_culture). Jobs often walked around the office barefoot even after Apple became a [*Fortune* 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) company. By the time of the "[1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_\(advertisement\))" television advertisement, Apple's informal culture had become a key trait that differentiated it from its competitors.[^cite_note-225] According to a 2011 report in *[Fortune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_\(magazine\))*, this has resulted in a corporate culture more akin to a startup rather than a multinational corporation.[^cite_note-Fortune1-226] In a 2017 interview, Wozniak credited watching *[Star Trek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek)* and attending [*Star Trek* conventions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek_convention) in his youth as inspiration for co-founding Apple.[^cite_note-227]

As the company has grown and been led by a series of differently opinionated chief executives, some media have suggested that it has lost some of its original character.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231] Nonetheless, it has maintained a reputation for fostering individuality and excellence that reliably attracts talented workers, particularly after Jobs returned.[^cite_note-232] Numerous Apple employees have stated that projects without Jobs' involvement often took longer than others.[^cite_note-cultofmacwork-233]

The Apple [Fellows](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fellow#Industry_and_corporate_fellows) program awards employees for extraordinary technical or leadership contributions to [personal computing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Personal_computing). Recipients include [Bill Atkinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Atkinson),[^cite_note-hertzfeld-234] [Steve Capps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Capps),[^cite_note-235] [Rod Holt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rod_Holt),[^cite_note-hertzfeld-234] [Alan Kay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Kay),[^cite_note-yoyow-236][^cite_note-237] [Guy Kawasaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guy_Kawasaki),[^cite_note-yoyow-236][^cite_note-238] [Al Alcorn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Alcorn),[^cite_note-239] [Don Norman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Norman),[^cite_note-yoyow-236] [Rich Page](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rich_Page),[^cite_note-hertzfeld-234] [Steve Wozniak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Wozniak),[^cite_note-hertzfeld-234] and [Phil Schiller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Schiller).[^cite_note-240]

Jobs intended that employees were to be specialists who are not exposed to functions outside their area of expertise. For instance, [Ron Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Johnson_\(businessman\))—Senior Vice President of Retail Operations until November 1, 2011—was responsible for site selection, in-store service, and store layout, yet had no control of the inventory in his stores. This was done by Tim Cook, who had a background in supply-chain management.[^cite_note-Lashinsky2011-241] Apple is known for strictly enforcing accountability. Each project has a "directly responsible individual" or "DRI" in Apple jargon.[^cite_note-Fortune1-226][^cite_note-242] Unlike other major US companies, Apple provides a relatively simple compensation policy for executives that does not include perks enjoyed by other CEOs like country club fees or private use of company aircraft. The company typically grants stock options to executives every other year.[^cite_note-243]

In 2015, Apple had 110,000 full-time employees. This increased to 116,000 full-time employees the next year, a notable hiring decrease, largely due to its first revenue decline. Apple does not specify how many of its employees work in retail, though its 2014 [SEC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Securities_and_Exchange_Commission) filing put the number at approximately half of its employee base.[^cite_note-244] In September 2017, Apple announced that it had over 123,000 full-time employees.[^cite_note-245]

Apple has a strong culture of [corporate secrecy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corporate_secrecy), and has an anti-[leak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News_leak) Global Security team that recruits from the [National Security Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Agency), the [Federal Bureau of Investigation](11127.md), and the [United States Secret Service](58221.md).[^cite_note-246] In December 2017, [Glassdoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glassdoor) said Apple was the 48th best place to work, having originally entered at rank 19 in 2009, peaking at rank 10 in 2012, and falling down the ranks in subsequent years.[^cite_note-247] In 2023, Bloomberg's [Mark Gurman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Gurman_\(journalist\)) revealed the existence of Apple's Exploratory Design Group (XDG), which was working to add glucose monitoring to the Apple Watch. Gurman compared XDG to Alphabet's [X "moonshot factory"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_Development).[^cite_note-248]

### Criticism and controversies

Main article: [Criticism of Apple Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criticism_of_Apple_Inc.)

Further information: [Apple litigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_litigation), [United States v. Apple (2024)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_v._Apple_\(2024\)), [Apple–EU tax dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple%E2%80%93EU_tax_dispute), [Foxconn suicides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foxconn_suicides), and [Apple in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_in_China)

Apple has faced criticism regarding its market practices, labor conditions, and privacy policies. The company has been criticized for alleged anti-competitive behavior[^cite_note-249] and its alleged participation in the [PRISM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PRISM) surveillance program.[^cite_note-250] Significant concerns have been raised about labor issues in [its supply chain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_supply_chain),[^cite_note-251] restrictions on the [right to repair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_to_repair),[^cite_note-252] and the company's tax strategies and environmental impact. Furthermore, Apple has faced scrutiny for its business operations in nations with restrictive [human rights records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_China), such as [China](5405.md).[^cite_note-253]

On May 5, 2026, Apple settled for \$250 million a shareholder lawsuit after the lawsuit was filed by Peter Landsheft in US federal court in California in 2024 over delays in AI upgrades to Siri.[^cite_note-254]

### Offices



[Apple Infinite Loop campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Infinite_Loop_campus) in Cupertino



[Apple Wolfe campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Wolfe_campus) in Sunnyvale

Main articles: [Apple Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Park), [Apple Infinite Loop campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Infinite_Loop_campus), and [Apple Wolfe campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Wolfe_campus)

Apple's headquarters are located at [Apple Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Park) in [Cupertino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cupertino), in the center of [Silicon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Valley). Opened in 2017, the circular [groundscraper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groundscraper) houses more than 12,000 employees. Apple co-founder [Steve Jobs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Jobs) envisioned the campus as resembling a nature preserve rather than a conventional office park, and presented the proposal to the Cupertino City Council in June 2011 during his final public appearance before his death.

Apple also operates the [Infinite Loop campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Infinite_Loop_campus), approximately 1 mile (1.6 km) west of Apple Park.[^cite_note-255] Opened in 1993 as a research and development campus, it served as Apple's headquarters from 1997 until the opening of Apple Park in 2017 and continues to house offices and laboratories.

In nearby [Sunnyvale, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunnyvale,_California), Apple occupies the [Wolfe campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Wolfe_campus), which was completed in 2018 after the company acquired the office development during construction.

In addition to these campuses, Apple occupies numerous office and research buildings throughout Cupertino and Sunnyvale, including the former headquarters buildings Stephens Creek 3 (1977–1978) and Mariani 1 (1982–1993).[^cite_note-256]

Apple's headquarters for Europe, the Middle East and Africa (EMEA) are located in [Cork](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cork_\(city\)) in the south of [Ireland](14560.md), called the Hollyhill campus.[^cite_note-257] The facility, which opened in 1980, houses 5,500 people and was Apple's first location outside of the United States.[^cite_note-258] Apple's international sales and distribution arms operate out of the campus in Cork.[^cite_note-259]

Apple has two campuses near Austin, Texas: a 216,000-square-foot (20,100 m<sup>2</sup>) campus opened in 2014 houses 500 engineers who work on [Apple silicon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_silicon)[^cite_note-260] and a 1.1-million-square-foot (100,000 m<sup>2</sup>) campus opened in 2021 where 6,000 people work in technical support, supply chain management, online store curation, and [Apple Maps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Maps) data management. The company also has several other major locations in [Boulder, Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boulder,_Colorado), [Culver City, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culver_City,_California), [Elk Grove, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elk_Grove,_California), [Herzliya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herzliya), [London](17867.md), [Los Angeles](18110.md), [Munich](19058.md), [San Diego](28504.md), [Seattle](11388236.md), and [Shanghai](27643.md) that each employ hundreds of people.[^cite_note-261]

### Litigation

Main article: [Litigation involving Apple Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Litigation_involving_Apple_Inc.)

Apple has been a participant in various legal proceedings and claims since it began operation.[^cite_note-262] In particular, Apple is known for and promotes itself as actively and aggressively enforcing its intellectual property interests. Some litigation examples include *[Apple v. Samsung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_v._Samsung)*, *[Apple v. Microsoft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_v._Microsoft)*, *[Motorola Mobility v. Apple Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorola_Mobility_v._Apple_Inc.)*, and *[Apple Corps v. Apple Computer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Corps_v._Apple_Computer)*. Apple has also had to defend itself against charges on numerous occasions of violating intellectual property rights. Most have been dismissed in the courts as [shell companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shell_companies) known as [patent trolls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patent_troll), with no evidence of actual use of [patents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patent) in question.[^cite_note-263] On December 21, 2016, Nokia announced that in the US and Germany, it has filed a suit against Apple, claiming that the latter's products infringe on Nokia's patents.[^cite_note-264]

Most recently, in November 2017, the [United States International Trade Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_International_Trade_Commission) announced an investigation into allegations of patent infringement in regards to Apple's remote desktop technology; Aqua Connect, a company that builds remote desktop software, has claimed that Apple infringed on two of its patents.[^cite_note-265]

[Epic Games](573199.md) filed a [lawsuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epic_Games_v._Apple) against Apple in August 2020 in the [United States District Court for the Northern District of California](891692.md), related to Apple's practices in the [App Store for iOS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/App_Store_\(Apple\)).

In January 2022, [Ericsson](9424.md) sued Apple over payment of royalty of [5G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5G) technology.[^cite_note-266] On June 24, 2024, the [European Commission](9974.md) accused Apple of violating the [Digital Markets Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_Markets_Act) by preventing "app developers from freely steering consumers to alternative channels for offers and content".[^cite_note-267] In April 2025, Apple was found guilty and fined €500 million (\$570 million) for violating the Digital Markets Act.[^cite_note-268]

In September 2026, Apple faced a proposed £2 billion collection action in the UK [Competition Appeal Tribunal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Competition_Appeal_Tribunal) concerning its App Tracking Transparency framework, with thousands of app developers alleging that the rules disadvantaged third-party developers.[^cite_note-269]

### Lobbying

In 2025, Apple was one of the donors who funded the White House's [East Wing demolition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Wing_demolition) and planned building of [a ballroom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House_State_Ballroom).[^cite_note-270]

## Finances

See also: [List of mergers and acquisitions by Apple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mergers_and_acquisitions_by_Apple)

| Country | Share |
| --- | --- |
| [Americas](29833.md) | 43.7% |
| [EMEA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EMEA) | 25.9% |
| [Greater China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_China) | 17.1% |
| Rest of [Asia-Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia-Pacific) | 7.8% |
| Japan | 6.1% |

As of 2024, Apple was the world's [fourth-largest personal computer vendor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_share_of_personal_computer_vendors#Current_top_vendors_market_share_\(2023\)),[^cite_note-272] the [largest vendor of tablet computers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tablet_computer#By_manufacturer),[^cite_note-273] and the [largest vendor of mobile phones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_best-selling_mobile_phones#2024).[^cite_note-274] It is a [Big Tech](57174306.md) company.[^cite_note-275][^cite_note-276]

In its fiscal year ending in September 2011, Apple reported a total of \$108 billion in annual revenues—a significant increase from its 2010 revenues of \$65 billion—and nearly \$82 billion in [cash reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cash_reserves).[^cite_note-277] On March 19, 2012, Apple announced plans for a \$2.65-per-share [dividend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dividend) beginning in the fourth quarter of 2012, per approval by its board of directors.[^cite_note-dividendfaq-278]

The company's worldwide annual revenue in 2013 totaled \$170 billion.[^cite_note-279] In May 2013, Apple entered the top ten of the [*Fortune* 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) list of companies for the first time, rising 11 places above its 2012 ranking to take the sixth position.[^cite_note-280] As of 2016, Apple has around US\$234 billion of cash and marketable securities, of which 90% is located outside the United States for tax purposes.[^cite_note-b200-281]

Apple amassed 65% of all profits made by the eight largest worldwide smartphone manufacturers in quarter one of 2014, according to a report by [Canaccord Genuity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canaccord_Genuity). In the first quarter of 2015, the company garnered 92% of all earnings.[^cite_note-282]

On April 30, 2017, *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* reported that Apple had [cash reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cash_reserve) of \$250 billion,[^cite_note-283] officially confirmed by Apple as specifically \$256.8 billion a few days later.[^cite_note-284]

As of August 3, 2018, Apple was the [largest publicly traded corporation in the world by market capitalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_corporations_by_market_capitalization). On August 2, 2018, Apple became the first publicly traded US company to reach a \$1 trillion market value,[^cite_note-1t-CNBC-285][^cite_note-1t-Guardian-286] and, as of October 2025, is valued at just over \$4 trillion.[^cite_note-287] Apple was ranked No. 3 on the 2023 [*Fortune* 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) rankings of the [largest United States corporations by revenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_companies_in_the_United_States_by_revenue).[^cite_note-288]

In July 2022, Apple reported an 11% decline in Q3 profits compared to 2021. Its revenue in the same period rose 2% year-on-year to \$83 billion, though this figure was also lower than in 2021, where the increase was at 36%. The general downturn is reportedly caused by the slowing global economy and supply chain disruptions in China.[^cite_note-289] That year, Apple was one of the [largest corporate spenders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_companies_by_research_and_development_spending) on research and development worldwide, with R\&D expenditure amounting to over \$27 billion.[^cite_note-290]

In May 2023, Apple reported a decline in its sales for the first quarter of 2023. Compared to that of 2022, revenue for 2023 fell by 3%. This is Apple's second consecutive quarter of sales decline. This fall is attributed to the slowing economy and consumers putting off purchases of iPads and computers due to increased pricing. However, iPhone sales held up with a year-on-year increase of 1.5%. According to Apple, demands for such devices were strong, particularly in Latin America and South Asia.[^cite_note-291]

### Taxes

Apple has created subsidiaries in low-tax places such as [Ireland](14560.md), the [Netherlands](21148.md), [Luxembourg](17515.md), and the [British Virgin Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Virgin_Islands) to cut the taxes it pays around the world. According to *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, in the 1980s Apple was among the first tech companies to designate overseas salespeople in high-tax countries in a manner that allowed the company to sell on behalf of low-tax subsidiaries on other continents, sidestepping income taxes. In the late 1980s, Apple was a pioneer of an accounting technique known as the "[Double Irish with a Dutch sandwich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double_Irish_with_a_Dutch_sandwich)", which reduces taxes by routing profits through Irish subsidiaries and the Netherlands and then to the Caribbean.[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293]

[British Conservative Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Conservative_Party) Member of Parliament [Charlie Elphicke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Elphicke) published research on October 30, 2012,[^cite_note-294] which showed that some multinational companies, including Apple, were making billions of pounds of profit in the UK, but were paying an effective tax rate to the UK Treasury of only 3 percent, well below standard [corporate tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corporate_tax) rates. He followed this research by calling on the [Chancellor of the Exchequer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chancellor_of_the_Exchequer) [George Osborne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Osborne) to force these multinationals, which also included [Google](1092923.md) and [the Coca-Cola Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Coca-Cola_Company), to state the effective rate of tax it pays on its UK revenues. Elphicke also said that government contracts should be withheld from multinationals who do not pay their fair share of UK tax.[^cite_note-295]

According to a [US Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Senate) report on the company's offshore tax structure concluded in May 2013, Apple has held billions of dollars in profits in Irish subsidiaries to pay little or no taxes to any government by using an unusual global tax structure.[^cite_note-296] The main subsidiary, a holding company that includes Apple's retail stores throughout Europe, has not paid any corporate income tax in the last five years. "Apple has exploited a difference between Irish and U.S. tax residency rules", the report said.[^cite_note-297] On May 21, 2013, Apple CEO [Tim Cook](46878774.md) defended his company's tax tactics at a Senate hearing.[^cite_note-298]

Apple says that it is the single largest taxpayer in the US, with an [effective tax rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_rate) of approximately of 26% as of Q2 [FY](5752099.md)2016.[^cite_note-299] In an interview with the German newspaper *[FAZ](513356.md)* in October 2017, Tim Cook stated that Apple was the biggest taxpayer worldwide.[^cite_note-300]

In 2016, after [a two-year investigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ireland_v_Commission), the European Commission claimed that Apple's use of a hybrid [Double Irish tax arrangement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double_Irish_arrangement) constituted "illegal state aid" from Ireland, and ordered Apple to pay €13 billion (\$14.5 billion) in unpaid taxes, the largest corporate tax fine in history. This was later annulled, after the [European General Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_General_Court) ruled that the commission had provided insufficient evidence.[^cite_note-301][^cite_note-IT11-302] In 2018, Apple repatriated \$285 billion to the United States, resulting in a \$38-billion tax payment spread over the following eight years.[^cite_note-303]

### Charity

Apple is a partner of [Product Red](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Product_Red), a fundraising campaign for [AIDS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIDS) charity. In November 2014, Apple arranged for all [App Store](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/App_Store_\(iOS\)) revenue in a two-week period to go to the fundraiser,[^cite_note-304] generating more than US\$20 million,[^cite_note-305] and in March 2017, it released an [iPhone 7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPhone_7) with a red color finish.[^cite_note-306] As of 2021, Apple has donated over \$250 million to Product Red.[^cite_note-307]

Apple contributes financially to fundraisers in times of natural disasters. In November 2012, it donated \$2.5 million to the [American Red Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Red_Cross) to aid relief efforts after [Hurricane Sandy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Sandy),[^cite_note-308] and in 2017 it donated \$5 million to relief efforts for both [Hurricane Irma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Irma) and [Hurricane Harvey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Harvey),[^cite_note-309] and for the [2017 Central Mexico earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Central_Mexico_earthquake).[^cite_note-310] The company has used its [iTunes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ITunes) platform to encourage donations in the wake of environmental disasters and humanitarian crises, such as the [2010 Haiti earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Haiti_earthquake),[^cite_note-311] the [2011 Japan earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Japan_earthquake),[^cite_note-312] [Typhoon Haiyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhoon_Haiyan) in the Philippines in November 2013,[^cite_note-313] and the [2015 European migrant crisis](46415102.md).[^cite_note-314] Apple emphasizes that it does not incur any processing or other fees for iTunes donations, sending 100% of the payments directly to relief efforts, though it also acknowledges that the Red Cross does not receive any personal information on the users donating and that the payments may not be tax deductible.[^cite_note-315]

On April 14, 2016, Apple and the [World Wide Fund for Nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Wide_Fund_for_Nature) (WWF) announced that they have engaged in a partnership to, "help protect life on our planet". Apple released a special page in its [App Store](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/App_Store_\(Apple\)), Apps for Earth. In the arrangement, Apple has committed that through April 24, WWF will receive 100% of the proceeds from the applications participating in the App Store via both the purchases of any paid apps and the In-App Purchases. Apple and WWF's Apps for Earth campaign raised more than \$8 million in total proceeds to support WWF's conservation work. WWF announced the results at WWDC 2016 in San Francisco.[^cite_note-316]

During the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), Apple's CEO Cook announced that the company would be donating "millions" of masks to health workers in the United States and Europe.[^cite_note-317] On January 13, 2021, Apple announced a \$100-million Racial Equity and Justice Initiative to help combat [institutional racism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institutional_racism) worldwide after the 2020 [murder of George Floyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murder_of_George_Floyd).[^cite_note-Doubles-318][^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320] In June 2023, Apple announced that it was doubling this and then distributed more than \$200 million to support organizations focused on education, economic growth, and criminal justice. Half is philanthropic grants and half is centered on equity.[^cite_note-Doubles-318]

## Environment

Main article: [Environmental impact of Apple Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_impact_of_Apple_Inc.)

### Apple Energy

Apple Energy, LLC is a wholly owned subsidiary of Apple that sells [solar energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_energy). As of June 6, 2016, Apple's solar farms in California and Nevada have been declared to provide 217.9 megawatts of solar generation capacity.[^cite_note-321] Apple has received regulatory approval to construct a [landfill gas energy plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landfill_gas_utilization) in [North Carolina](21650.md) to use the [methane emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methane_emissions) to generate electricity.[^cite_note-322] Apple's North Carolina data center is already powered entirely by renewable sources.[^cite_note-323]

### Energy and resources

In 2010, [Climate Counts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_Counts), a nonprofit organization dedicated to directing consumers toward the greenest companies, gave Apple a score of 52 points out of a possible 100, which puts Apple in its top category "Striding".[^cite_note-324] This was an increase from May 2008, when Climate Counts only gave Apple 11 points out of 100, which placed the company last among electronics companies, at which time Climate Counts also labeled Apple with a "stuck icon", adding that Apple at the time was "a choice to avoid for the climate-conscious consumer".[^cite_note-iwclimate-325]

Following a [Greenpeace](12233.md) protest, Apple released a statement on April 17, 2012, committing to ending its use of coal and shifting to 100% renewable clean energy.[^cite_note-After_Greenpeace_Protests,_Apple_Promises_to_Dump_Coal_Power-326][^cite_note-Powering_Our_Facilities_with_Clean,_Renewable_Energy-327] By 2013, Apple was using [100% renewable energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/100%25_renewable_energy) to power its [data centers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_centers). Overall, 75% of the company's power came from clean renewable sources.[^cite_note-328]

In May 2015, Greenpeace evaluated the state of the Green Internet and commended Apple on its environmental practices saying, "Apple's commitment to renewable energy has helped set a new bar for the industry, illustrating in very concrete terms that a 100% renewable Internet is within its reach, and providing several models of intervention for other companies that want to build a sustainable Internet."[^cite_note-329]

As of 2016, Apple states that 100% of its US operations run on [renewable energy](25784.md), 100% of Apple's data centers run on renewable energy and 93% of Apple's global operations run on renewable energy.[^cite_note-330] However, the facilities are connected to the local [grid](20344155.md) which usually contains a mix of fossil and renewable sources, so Apple uses [carbon offsets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_offset) for its electricity use.[^cite_note-331] The [Electronic Product Environmental Assessment Tool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_Product_Environmental_Assessment_Tool) (EPEAT) allows consumers to see the effect a product has on the environment. Each product receives a Gold, Silver, or Bronze rank depending on its efficiency and sustainability. Every Apple [tablet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tablet_computer), [notebook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laptop), [desktop computer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desktop_computer), and [display](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_monitor) that EPEAT ranks achieves a Gold rating, the highest possible. Although Apple's data centers recycle water 35 times,[^cite_note-332] the increased activity in retail, corporate and data centers also increase the amount of water use to 573 million US gal (2.2 million m<sup>3</sup>) in 2015.[^cite_note-333]

During an event on March 21, 2016, Apple provided a status update on its environmental initiative to be 100% renewable in all of its worldwide operations. [Lisa P. Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lisa_P._Jackson), Apple's vice president of Environment, Policy and Social Initiatives who reports directly to CEO, Tim Cook, announced that as of March 2016, 93% of Apple's worldwide operations are powered with renewable energy. Also featured was the company's efforts to use sustainable paper in its product packaging; 99% of all paper used by Apple in the product packaging comes from [post-consumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-consumer) [recycled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recycle) paper or sustainably managed forests, as the company continues its move to all paper packaging for all of its products.[^cite_note-334]

Apple announced on August 16, 2016, that Lens Technology, one of its major suppliers in China, has committed to power all its [glass production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glass_production) for Apple with 100 percent renewable energy by 2018. The commitment was a large step in Apple's efforts to help manufacturers lower their carbon footprint in China.[^cite_note-335] Apple also announced that all 14 of its final assembly sites in China are now compliant with [UL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UL_\(safety_organization\))'s Zero Waste to Landfill validation. The standard, which started in January 2015, certifies that all manufacturing waste is [reused](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuse), [recycled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recycled), [composted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compost), or converted into energy (when necessary). Since the program began, nearly 140,000 metric tons of waste have been diverted from [landfills](211285.md).[^cite_note-336]

On July 21, 2020, Apple announced its plan to become [carbon neutral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_neutral) across its entire business, manufacturing supply chain, and product life cycle by 2030. In the next 10 years, Apple will try to lower emissions with a series of innovative actions, including: low carbon product design, expanding energy efficiency, renewable energy, process and material innovations, and carbon removal.[^cite_note-337]

In June 2024, the [United States Environmental Protection Agency](58666.md) (EPA) published a report about an electronic computer manufacturing facility leased by Apple in 2015 in [Santa Clara, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Clara,_California), code named Aria.[^cite_note-Roscoe20240625-338][^cite_note-339] The EPA report stated that Apple was potentially in violation of federal regulations under the [Resource Conservation and Recovery Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resource_Conservation_and_Recovery_Act) (RCRA).[^cite_note-Roscoe20240625-338] According to a report from [*Bloomberg*](3057255.md) in 2018, the facility is used to develop [microLED](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MicroLED) screens under the code name T159.[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-Roscoe20240625-338][^cite_note-341] The inspection found that Apple was potentially mistreating waste as only subject to [California](5407.md) regulations and that it had potentially miscalculated the effectiveness of Apple's [activated carbon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Activated_carbon) filters, which filter [volatile organic compounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volatile_organic_compound) (VOCs) from the air. The EPA inspected the facility in August 2023 due to a tip from a former Apple employee who posted the report on [X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_\(social_media_platform\)).[^cite_note-Roscoe20240625-338]

### Toxins

Following further campaigns by Greenpeace,[^cite_note-342] in 2008, Apple became the first electronics manufacturer to eliminate all [polyvinyl chloride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polyvinyl_chloride) (PVC) and [brominated flame retardants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brominated_flame_retardant) (BFRs) in its complete product line.[^cite_note-343] In June 2007, Apple began replacing the [cold cathode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_cathode) fluorescent lamp (CCFL) backlit [LCDs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LCD) in its computers with [mercury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercury_\(element\))-free [LED-backlit LCD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LED-backlit_LCD) and [arsenic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arsenic)-free glass, starting with the upgraded [MacBook Pro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MacBook_Pro).[^cite_note-Apple-environmental-news-344][^cite_note-agreenapple-345][^cite_note-green_notebooks-346][^cite_note-347] Apple offers comprehensive and transparent information about the [CO<sub>2</sub>e](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_dioxide_equivalent), [emissions](9528025.md), materials, and [electrical usage](6168349.md) concerning every product it currently produces or has sold in the past (and which it has enough data needed to produce the report), in its portfolio on its homepage. Allowing consumers to make informed purchasing decisions on the products it offers for sale.[^cite_note-Apple-environment-reports-348] In June 2009, Apple's [iPhone 3GS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPhone_3GS) was free of PVC, arsenic, and BFRs.[^cite_note-Apple-environmental-news-344][^cite_note-349] Since 2009, all Apple products have mercury-free LED-backlit LCDs, arsenic-free glass, and non-PVC cables.[^cite_note-350] All Apple products have EPEAT Gold status and beat the latest Energy Star guidelines in each product's respective regulatory category.[^cite_note-Apple-environmental-news-344][^cite_note-351]

In November 2011, Apple was featured in Greenpeace's Guide to Greener Electronics, which ranks electronics manufacturers on sustainability, [climate and energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_and_energy) policy, and how "green" its products are. The company ranked fourth of fifteen electronics companies (moving up five places from the previous year) with a score of 4.6/10.[^cite_note-352] Greenpeace praised Apple's [sustainability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainability_metrics_and_indices), noting that the company exceeded its 70% global recycling goal in 2010. Apple continues to score well on product ratings, with all of its products now being free of PVC plastic and BFRs. However, the guide criticized Apple on the Energy criteria for not seeking external verification of its greenhouse gas emissions data, and for not setting any targets to reduce emissions.[^cite_note-353] In January 2012, Apple requested that its cable maker, Volex, begin producing [halogen-free](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Low_smoke_zero_halogen) [USB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USB) and power cables.[^cite_note-354]

### Green bonds

In February 2016, Apple issued a US\$1.5-billion [green bond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_bond) (climate bond), the first ever of its kind by a US tech company. The green bond proceeds are dedicated to the financing of environmental projects.[^cite_note-Apple-environment-reports-348][^cite_note-355]

## Supply chain

Main article: [Apple supply chain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_supply_chain)

See also: [List of Apple Inc. suppliers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Apple_Inc._suppliers)

As of 2018, Apple used hardware components from 43 different countries.[^cite_note-356]

Apple products were made in the United States in Apple-owned factories until the late 1990s; however, as a result of [outsourcing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outsourcing) initiatives in the 2000s, almost all of its manufacturing is now handled abroad. According to a report by *The New York Times*, Apple insiders "believe the vast scale of overseas factories, as well as the flexibility, diligence and industrial skills of foreign workers, have so outpaced their American counterparts that '[Made in the U.S.A.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Made_in_the_U.S.A.)' is no longer a viable option for most Apple products".[^cite_note-357] South Korean firms, including [Samsung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samsung) and [LG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LG), worked closely with Apple as parts partners for the [iMac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IMac), [iPod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPod), and [iPhone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPhone) before it increased its reliance on Taiwan and China.[^cite_note-McGee_2025-358]<sup>: 74–232 </sup> Taiwanese Apple suppliers such as [Foxconn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foxconn), [Wistron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wistron), [Pegatron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pegatron), [Quanta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quanta_Computer), and [Compal Electronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compal_Electronics) established a sizable manufacturing presence in China.[^cite_note-359] By the 2020s, orders were shifting to Chinese firms such as to [Luxshare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxshare), [BYD Electronic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Company), [Goertek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goertek), and [Wingtech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wingtech). By 2021, Apple had more suppliers from China than Taiwan.[^cite_note-McGee_2025-358]<sup>: 337 </sup> In March 2017, *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* reported that Apple would begin manufacturing [iPhone models](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPhone_models) in India "over the next two months",[^cite_note-360] and in May, the *Journal* wrote that an Apple manufacturer had begun production of [iPhone SE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPhone_SE_\(1st_generation\)) in the country,[^cite_note-361] In May 2017, the company announced a \$1-billion funding project for "advanced manufacturing" in the United States,[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] In April 2019, Apple initiated manufacturing of [iPhone 7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPhone_7) at its [Bengaluru](44275267.md) facility.[^cite_note-364]

The company's manufacturing, procurement, and logistics enable it to execute massive product launches without having to maintain large, profit-sapping inventories. In 2011, Apple's profit margins were 40 percent, compared with between 10 and 20 percent for most other hardware companies. Cook's catchphrase to describe his focus on the company's operational arm is: "Nobody wants to buy sour milk."[^cite_note-supply-365][^cite_note-366]

### Labor force

Main article: [Apple Inc. and unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Inc._and_unions)

Apple directly employs 147,000 workers including 25,000 corporate employees in [Apple Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Park) and across Silicon Valley.[^cite_note-Downey2021-367][^cite_note-Leswing2021-368] The vast majority of its employees work at the over 500 retail [Apple Stores](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Store) globally.[^cite_note-369] Apple relies on a larger, outsourced workforce for manufacturing, particularly in China where Apple directly employs 10,000 workers across its retail and corporate divisions. In addition, one further million workers are contracted by Apple's suppliers to assemble Apple products, including [Foxconn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foxconn) and [Pegatron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pegatron).[^cite_note-370] [Zhengzhou Technology Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhengzhou_Technology_Park) alone employs 350,000 Chinese workers in [Zhengzhou](288264.md) to exclusively work on the [iPhone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPhone).[^cite_note-371]

Apple workers around the globe have been involved in [organizing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organizing_model) since the 1990s.[^cite_note-Hyde2002-372] Apple unions are made up of retail, corporate, and outsourced workers. Apple employees have joined [trade unions](17626.md) or formed [works councils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Works_council) in [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Inc._and_unions#Australia),[^cite_note-Zhuang2022-373] [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Inc._and_unions#France),[^cite_note-374] [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Inc._and_unions#Germany),[^cite_note-Loehne2012-375] [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Inc._and_unions#Italy),[^cite_note-376] [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Inc._and_unions#Japan),[^cite_note-Corrales2022-377] the [United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Inc._and_unions#United_Kingdom)[^cite_note-378] and the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Inc._and_unions#United_States).[^cite_note-Albergotti2022-379] In 2021, [Apple Together](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Together), a [solidarity union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solidarity_union), sought to bring together the company's global worker organizations.[^cite_note-Harrington2022-380] The majority of industrial [labor disputes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_dispute) (including union recognition) involving Apple occur indirectly through [its suppliers and contractors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_supply_chain), notably [Foxconn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foxconn) plants in [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Inc._and_unions#China)[^cite_note-Chan2013-381] and, to a lesser extent, in [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Inc._and_unions#Brazil)[^cite_note-382] and [India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Inc._and_unions#India).[^cite_note-383]

Apple has been criticized for labor sourcing and conditions at the facilities of its contract manufacturers[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386] as well as mines.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388][^cite_note-389]
